I feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and the people at work. Even though I'm really just trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity at home with your family as much as you do at work or school?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't... I don't really compare the two. I guess it's just kind of... there. I haven't really paid attention to whether it's worse at home or at the school.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're constantly 'on the go' even during your free time or when you're relaxing?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Um... yeah, that's actually what brings me in. I just feel like I'm constantly running on a motor lately. Like, I'll finish one thing at school or... or at home, and I just immediately jump into the next thing. I just... I can't seem to shut myself off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're 'on the go' as much when you're alone as when you're with other people?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I guess I just... I haven't really noticed if it changes when I'm by myself. It's just sort of always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and constant activity happen more often in certain situations or settings?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any kind of pattern. It just feels like it's all the time, no matter where I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven't thought about it like that. I guess I just... I haven't really noticed if it changes when I'm by myself. It's just sort of always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and constant activity happen more often in certain situations or settings?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any kind of pattern. It just feels like it's all the time, no matter where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family due to your restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yeah. It's, um... it's been an issue. My spouse says things at home are harder now. Like with the caregiving routines, she feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else. I just feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and my colleagues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it's, um, always been like this. I just feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and my colleagues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it's, um, always been like this. My mother used to joke about it. Even back in elementary school, she'd say I... I tried to teach the class from my seat. I guess I was always getting out of my chair, or talking... interrupting the teacher before they even finished giving directions. \n\nSo... yeah. It's not something that just started recently with all the changes in my family. It's just... part of who I've always been, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take time off work or significantly reduce your work or social activities because of these feelings of restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't taken any time off. I just... I keep going in to work. I haven't really stopped doing anything, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these feelings of restlessness and constant activity consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose... I haven't really kept track of it year by year. But... yeah, I guess it's mostly just always been there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't really notice it until, well, until people start glancing over at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, my job involves a lot of paperwork and tracking student progress, so I have to be pretty careful. I haven't really noticed making a lot of careless mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of restlessness and constant activity causing you a lot of distress or problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. It's caused some problems. Um... at work, people have complained. It actually got to the point where they put me on a corrective plan, which... wasn't great. And at home... my spouse says our caregiving routines are harder. Like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we have going on. \n\nIt's just... I don't know. I feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and the people at work. Even though I'm really just trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity at home with your family as much as you do at work or school?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't... I don't really compare the two. I guess it's just kind of... there. I haven't really paid attention to whether it's worse at home or at the school.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're constantly 'on the go' even during your free time or when you're relaxing?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Um... yeah, that's actually what brings me in. I just feel like I'm constantly running on a motor lately. Like, I'll finish one thing at school or... or at home, and I just immediately jump into the next thing. I just... I can't seem to shut myself off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're 'on the go' as much when you're alone as when you're with other people?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I guess I just... I haven't really noticed if it changes when I'm by myself. It's just sort of always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and constant activity happen more often in certain situations or settings?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any kind of pattern. It just feels like it's all the time, no matter where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family due to your restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yeah. It's, um... it's been an issue. My spouse says things at home are harder now. Like with the caregiving routines, she feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else. And... well, it's at work, too. The complaints there got serious enough that they put me on a corrective plan. \n\nI'm really just trying to help, but... I don't know. I just feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and my colleagues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it's, um, always been like this. My mother used to joke about it. Even back in elementary school, she'd say I... I tried to teach the class from my seat. I guess I was always getting out of my chair, or talking... interrupting the teacher before they even finished giving directions. \n\nSo... yeah. It's not something that just started recently with all the changes in my family. It's just... part of who I've always been, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take time off work or significantly reduce your work or social activities because of these feelings of restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't taken any time off. I just... I keep going in to work. I haven't really stopped doing anything, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these feelings of restlessness and constant activity consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose... I haven't really kept track of it year by year. But... yeah, I guess it's mostly just always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel restless or constantly 'on the go'?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't think there was ever a time like that. It's just... always been this way, as far as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when doing group activities?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I just... wait my turn, I guess. I haven't really noticed an issue with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess I do. I don't really mean to. Just... if my colleagues or the parents at school are talking... or my wife and her relatives... I kind of just jump in. I always think I'm helping out, but... I suppose I end up talking over them. Taking charge before they even get a chance to finish what they were saying.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just sort of always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and constant activity happen more often in certain situations or settings?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I haven't noticed any kind of pattern. It just feels like it's all the time, no matter where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family due to your restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yeah. It's, um... it's been an issue. My spouse says things at home are harder now. Like with the caregiving routines, she feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else. And... well, it's at work, too. The complaints there got serious enough that they put me on a corrective plan. \n\nI'm really just trying to help, but... I don't know. I just feel like I'm damaging trust with my family and my colleagues.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these feelings of restlessness and constant activity when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it's, um, always been like this. My mother used to joke about it. Even back in elementary school, she'd say I... I tried to teach the class from my seat. I guess I was always getting out of my chair, or talking... interrupting the teacher before they even finished giving directions. \n\nSo... yeah. It's not something that just started recently with all the changes in my family. It's just... part of who I've always been, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take time off work or significantly reduce your work or social activities because of these feelings of restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't taken any time off. I just... I keep going in to work. I haven't really stopped doing anything, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these feelings of restlessness and constant activity consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose... I haven't really kept track of it year by year. But... yeah, I guess it's mostly just always been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel restless or constantly 'on the go'?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't think there was ever a time like that. It's just... always been this way, as far as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when doing group activities?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I just... wait my turn, I guess. I haven't really noticed an issue with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess I do. I don't really mean to. Just... if my colleagues or the parents at school are talking... or my wife and her relatives... I kind of just jump in. I always think I'm helping out, but... I suppose I end up talking over them. Taking charge before they even get a chance to finish what they were saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant problems with relationships or work performance that you attribute directly to your restlessness and constant activity?\n\nPatient: I... suppose so, yeah. It's... not easy to talk about. At the school, the complaints got serious enough that they actually put me on a corrective plan. \n\nAnd... at home, my wife says I make things harder. She tells me that our caregiving routines are more difficult because they have to manage my energy on top of everything else. I just... I feel like I'm damaging trust with my colleagues and my family. Even though I'm really just... trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your main problem is that you can't focus or that you can't stay still?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I'm not really sure how to separate them. I just know I'm always moving.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
